[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Design a module named fp_mul_e5f10 that multiplies two floating-point numbers. Format: 16-bit (1 sign + 5 exponent + 10 fraction). Inputs: a and b (16-bit each). Output: y (16-bit). Subnormal numbers must be supported.

Required behaviour, bit-exact: subnormal inputs are used at their true value; results are rounded to nearest, ties to even, including results in the subnormal range; a NaN input and inf x 0 give the canonical quiet NaN 16'h7E00; inf x finite gives inf with the product's sign; an overflowing product gives inf; a zero product gives a signed zero. The testbench enumerates about 18.9 million operand pairs against a reference implementation.

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` in your workspace, a plain readable implementation (unpack, significand multiply, normalise, round, pack) that is correct. Optimize from there.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.

module fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);
  input  [15:0] a;
  input  [15:0] b;
  output [15:0] y;

  localparam BIAS         = 15;
  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN
  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;
  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product

  // 1. unpack
  wire       s_a = a[15];
  wire       s_b = b[15];
  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];
  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];
  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];
  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];

  // 2. classify
  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);
  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);
  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);
  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);
  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);
  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);
  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);
  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);

  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);
  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;
  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;
  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;

  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)
  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};
  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};
  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;
  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;

  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;

  reg [4:0] lz;
  integer   i;
  always @* begin
    lz = 5'd0;
    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)
      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];
  end

  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;
  genvar k;
  generate
    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below
      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];
    end
  endgenerate

  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector
    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;
    input integer      idx;
    begin
      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;
    end
  endfunction

  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10
  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);
  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)
  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);
  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];

  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out
  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard
  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even

  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};
  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one
  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];
  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];

  // 6. exponent of a normal result
  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};
  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};
  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};
  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};
  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result
  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number
  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;
  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];

  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once
  integer           total_shift;
  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;
  always @* begin
    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});
    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};
  end
  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];
  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);
  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);
  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);
  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};
  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal
  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];
  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};

  // 8. pack
  wire is_nan      = nan_in;
  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));
  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;
  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);
  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);

  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;
  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;
  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;

  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};
endmodule

[Step 2/60]
`timescale 1ns/10ps
// exhaustive-directed tb (fpmul_run)
// Golden-referenced directed testbench: compares the DUT against the embedded
// golden reference (fp_mul_e5f10_ref, generated from context/starting_point.py)
// over ~18.9M enumerated vectors — no stimuli file. Structure:
//   1. the 3 known subnormal-boundary regression pairs (+ golden self-checks)
//   2. every subnormal/zero a (2^11 sign+mantissa patterns) x 4096 structured b
//   3. 4096 structured a x every subnormal/zero b
//   4. 2^21 LFSR-driven broad pairs
// Structured operands: both signs x all 32 exponents x 64 mantissas
// (8 corners + 56 LFSR-derived). The retired file-driven testbench is kept at
// tb_vectors.sv (+ vectors.dat) for cross-checking; it is not compiled.
module tb;
  logic [15:0] a, b, y, y_ref;
  int errors;
  longint total;

  fp_mul_e5f10 dut (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y));
  fp_mul_e5f10_ref refm (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y_ref));

  function automatic [15:0] lfsr_next(input [15:0] s);
    lfsr_next = (s >> 1) ^ (s[0] ? 16'hB400 : 16'h0000);
  endfunction

  logic [15:0] structured [0:4095];
  localparam logic [9:0] MANT_CORNERS [0:7] =
      '{10'h000, 10'h001, 10'h002, 10'h003, 10'h3FF, 10'h3FE, 10'h2AA, 10'h155};

  task automatic check();
    #1;
    total = total + 1;
    if (y !== y_ref) begin
      if (errors < 10)
        $display("TB_ERROR a=%0d b=%0d expected_y=%0d actual_y=%0d", a, b, y_ref, y);
      errors = errors + 1;
    end
  endtask

  logic [15:0] lfsr, ra, rb;
  logic [9:0] mant;
  int idx;

  initial begin
    errors = 0; total = 0;

    lfsr = 16'hACE1;
    idx = 0;
    for (int s = 0; s < 2; s++)
      for (int e = 0; e < 32; e++)
        for (int m = 0; m < 64; m++) begin
          if (m < 8) mant = MANT_CORNERS[m];
          else begin lfsr = lfsr_next(lfsr); mant = lfsr[9:0]; end
          structured[idx] = {s[0], e[4:0], mant};
          idx = idx + 1;
        end

[ASSISTANT]
The starting point passes at cost ~145429 (area 108.7, delay 1338). The critical path goes through the multiplier, then the LZC/priority logic, barrel shifters and rounding. My plan: restructure with **input pre-normalization** (LZC+shift on the 11-bit operands before the multiply, off the product's critical path), leaving only a 1-bit normalize after the product, a single normal rounding adder, and a compact subnormal path.
  -> create_file(filename='v1.sv', content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1/5/10) with subnormals.\n// Architecture: pre-normalise operand significands (LZC + left shift),\n// 11x11 multiply, 0/1-bit normalise, round-to-nearest-even.\n// Subnormal results share the same product with a single right shift.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // ---- classify ----\n  wire        sa = a[15],    sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10], eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0],   fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0),  eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eap = (ea == 5'd31), ebp = (eb == 5'd31);\n\n  wire zero_a = ea0 & (fa == 10'd0), zero_b = eb0 & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = eap & (fa == 10'd0), inf_b  = ebp & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_in = (eap & (fa != 10'd0)) | (ebp & (fb != 10'd0))\n              | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire sy      = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- significands + pre-normalisation ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  wire [3:0] za = ma[10] ? 4'd0  : ma[9] ? 4'd1  : ma[8] ? 4'd2 : ma[7] ? 4'd3 :\n                  ma[6]  ? 4'd4  : ma[5] ? 4'd5  : ma[4] ? 4'd6 : ma[3] ? 4'd7 :\n                  ma[2]  ? 4'd8  : ma[1] ? 4'd9  : ma[0] ? 4'd10 : 4'd11;\n  wire [3:0] zb = mb[10] ? 4'd0  : mb[9] ? 4'd1  : mb[8] ? 4'd2 : mb[7] ? 4'd3 :\n                  mb[6]  ? 4'd4  : mb[5] ? 4'd5  : mb[4] ? 4'd6 : mb[3] ? 4'd7 :\n                  mb[2]  ? 4'd8  : mb[1] ? 4'd9  : mb[0] ? 4'd10 : 4'd11;\n\n  reg [10:0] man, mbn;\n  integer k;\n  always @* begin\n    man = 11'd0;\n    for (k = 0; k <= 10; k = k + 1)\n      if (za == k[3:0]) man = ma << k;\n  end\n  always @* begin\n    mbn = 11'd0;\n    for (k = 0; k <= 10; k = k + 1)\n      if (zb == k[3:0]) mbn = mb << k;\n  end\n\n  wire signed [6:0] ean  = (ea0 ? 7'sd1 : $signed({2'b00, ea})) - $signed({3'b000, za});\n  wire signed [6:0] ebn  = (eb0 ? 7'sd1 : $signed({2'b00, eb})) - $signed({3'b000, zb});\n  wire signed [6:0] esum = ean + ebn;\n\n  // ---- product, 1-bit normalisation ----\n  wire [21:0] prod = man * mbn;\n  wire        s    = prod[21];\n  wire [20:0] w    = s ? prod[21:1] : prod[20:0];   // w[20] = 1 for nonzero\n\n  // E = exponent of normalised product result (value = (w/2^20) * 2^(E-15))\n  wire signed [6:0] eprod = esum + $signed({6'b000000, s}) - 7'sd15;\n  wire signed [6:0] j     = -eprod;        // right shift for subnormal result\n  wire juse = ~j[6] & (j <= 7'sd10);       // 0 <= j <= 10\n\n  // ---- normal result: round w at 11-bit precision ----\n  wire [10:0] rpre   = w[20:10];\n  wire        gn     = w[9];\n  wire        stn    = |w[8:0];\n  wire        rup    = gn & (stn | rpre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] rres   = {1'b0, rpre} + {11'd0, rup};\n  wire        cr     = rres[11];                     // rounded up to 2^11\n  wire signed [6:0] er = eprod + $signed({6'b000000, cr});\n  wire ovf = ~er[6] & (er > 7'sd30);                 // er >= 31 -> overflow\n\n  // ---- subnormal result: frac = round(w / 2^(11+j)) ----\n  reg [20:0] z;\n  always @* begin\n    z = 21'd0;\n    if (juse)\n      for (k = 0; k <= 10; k = k + 1)\n        if (j[3:0] == k[3:0]) z = w >> k;\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  t10  = z[20:11];\n  wire        gs   = z[10];\n  wire        sts  = |z[9:0];\n  wire        rs   = gs & (sts | t10[0]);\n  wire [10:0] fres = {1'b0, t10} + {10'd0, rs};\n  wire        cs   = fres[10];                       // rounded up to 2^10\n\n  // ---- pack ----\n  wire sub     = ~j[6];                              // E <= 0\n  wire is_nan  = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf  = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (~sub & ovf & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_zero = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | (sub & ~cs & (fres[9:0] == 10'd0)));\n  wire is_sub  = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & ~is_zero & sub;\n\n  assign y = is_nan  ? 16'h7E00 :\n             is_inf  ? {sy, 5'd31, 10'd0} :\n             is_zero ? {sy, 5'd0,  10'd0} :\n             is_sub  ? {sy, 5'd1,  fres[9:0]} :\n                       {sy, er[4:0], rres[9:0]};\nendmodule\n")

[ASSISTANT]
The target_delay knob has no effect on mapping here. Critical path analysis suggests my front-end (nested-ternary LZC + 11-way shifters) is the main delay contributor, plus a deep tail. Let me build v2 with: (1) a 3-level tree LZC + 4-stage binary shifters, (2) exponent math restructured around a single `jv` quantity, (3) narrower 12-bit subnormal window shifter.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- binary16 multiplier, pre-normalising architecture, v2.\n// v2 changes vs v1: 3-level tree LZC + 4-stage binary operand shifters,\n// exponent arithmetic restructured around jv = -E (single subtract from\n// parallel E0/ZS), 12-bit window shifter for the subnormal path.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // ---- classify ----\n  wire        sa = a[15],    sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10], eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0],   fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0),  eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eap = (ea == 5'd31), ebp = (eb == 5'd31);\n\n  wire zero_a = ea0 & (fa == 10'd0), zero_b = eb0 & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = eap & (fa == 10'd0), inf_b  = ebp & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_in = (eap & (fa != 10'd0)) | (ebp & (fb != 10'd0))\n              | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire sy      = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- significands ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  // ---- 3-level tree leading-zero count (binary, 0..11) ----\n  wire        g0nz = ma[10] | ma[9],  g1nz = ma[8] | ma[7];\n  wire        g2nz = ma[6] | ma[5],   g3nz = ma[4] | ma[3];\n  wire        g4nz = ma[2] | ma[1],   g5nz = ma[0];\n  wire        g0z = ~ma[10], g1z = ~ma[8], g2z = ~ma[6], g3z = ~ma[4], g4z = ~ma[2];\n  wire        h0nz = g0nz | g1nz,  wire [1:0] h0z = g0nz ? {1'b0, g0z} : {1'b1, g1z};\n  wire        h1nz = g2nz | g3nz,  wire [1:0] h1z = g2nz ? {1'b0, g2z} : {1'b1, g3z};\n  wire        h2nz = g4nz | g5nz,  wire [1:0] h2z = g4nz ? {1'b0, g4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        f0nz = h0nz | h1nz,  wire [2:0] f0z = h0nz ? {1'b0, h0z} : {1'b1, h1z};\n  wire [3:0]  za = f0nz ? {1'b0, f0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, h2z});\n\n  wire        k0nz = mb[10] | mb[9],  k1nz = mb[8] | mb[7];\n  wire        k2nz = mb[6] | mb[5],   k3nz = mb[4] | mb[3];\n  wire        k4nz = mb[2] | mb[1],   k5nz = mb[0];\n  wire        k0z = ~mb[10], k1z = ~mb[8], k2z = ~mb[6], k3z = ~mb[4], k4z = ~mb[2];\n  wire        l0nz = k0nz | k1nz,  wire [1:0] l0z = k0nz ? {1'b0, k0z} : {1'b1, k1z};\n  wire        l1nz = k2nz | k3nz,  wire [1:0] l1z = k2nz ? {1'b0, k2z} : {1'b1, k3z};\n  wire        l2nz = k4nz | k5nz,  wire [1:0] l2z = k4nz ? {1'b0, k4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        m0nz = l0nz | l1nz,  wire [2:0] m0z = l0nz ? {1'b0, l0z} : {1'b1, l1z};\n  wire [3:0]  zb = m0nz ? {1'b0, m0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, l2z});\n\n  // ---- 4-stage binary left shifters (11 bits, drop overflow) ----\n  wire [10:0] sa1 = za[0] ? {ma[9:0],  1'b0} : ma;\n  wire [10:0] sa2 = za[1] ? {sa1[8:0], 2'b0} : sa1;\n  wire [10:0] sa4 = za[2] ? {sa2[6:0], 4'b0} : sa2;\n  wire [10:0] man = za[3] ? {sa4[2:0], 8'b0} : sa4;\n\n  wire [10:0] sb1 = zb[0] ? {mb[9:0],  1'b0} : mb;\n  wire [10:0] sb2 = zb[1] ? {sb1[8:0], 2'b0} : sb1;\n  wire [10:0] sb4 = zb[2] ? {sb2[6:0], 4'b0} : sb2;\n  wire [10:0] mbn = zb[3] ? {sb4[2:0], 8'b0} : sb4;\n\n  // ---- exponent base: jv = -E = 15 - E0 + ZS - s, E0 = sum of raw exps ----\n  wire [5:0]  E0  = {1'b0, (ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea)} + {1'b0, (eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb)};\n  wire [4:0]  ZS  = {1'b0, za} + {1'b0, zb};\n  wire [6:0]  jbase = 7'd15 - {1'b0, E0} + {2'd0, ZS};\n\n  // ---- product, 1-bit normalisation ----\n  wire [21:0] prod = man * mbn;\n  wire        s    = prod[21];\n  wire [20:0] w    = s ? prod[21:1] : prod[20:0];\n  wire        tail = s & prod[0];\n\n  wire [6:0]  jv   = jbase - {6'd0, s};   // jv = -E ; >=0 -> subnormal result\n\n  // ---- normal path: round w at 11-bit precision ----\n  wire [10:0] rpre   = w[20:10];\n  wire        gn     = w[9];\n  wire        stn    = (|w[8:0]) | tail;\n  wire        rup    = gn & (stn | rpre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] rres   = {1'b0, rpre} + {11'd0, rup};\n  wire        cr     = rres[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n = rres[9:0];\n\n  // ---- shared exponent / selection from jv ----\n  wire signed [6:0] expfs = $signed({6'b0, cr}) - $signed(jv);  // E + cr\n  wire sub  = ~jv[6];                       // E <= 0\n  wire ovf  = (expfs >= 7'sd31);            // E + cr >= 31\n  wire juse = (jv <= 7'd10);                // 0 <= j <= 10\n  wire [3:0] jj = jv[3:0];\n\n  // ---- subnormal path: frac = round(w / 2^(11+j)) ----\n  wire [31:0] wx = {11'd0, w};\n  reg  [11:0] win;\n  always @* begin\n    win = 12'd0;\n    if (juse)\n      case (jj)\n        4'd0:  win = wx[10 +: 12];\n        4'd1:  win = wx[11 +: 12];\n        4'd2:  win = wx[12 +: 12];\n        4'd3:  win = wx[13 +: 12];\n        4'd4:  win = wx[14 +: 12];\n        4'd5:  win = wx[15 +: 12];\n        4'd6:  win = wx[16 +: 12];\n        4'd7:  win = wx[17 +: 12];\n        4'd8:  win = wx[18 +: 12];\n        4'd9:  win = wx[19 +: 12];\n        4'd10: win = wx[20 +: 12];\n        default: win = 12'd0;\n      endcase\n  end\n  wire        gs   = win[0];\n  wire [9:0]  t10  = win[11:1];\n  // sticky = |w[9+j:0] = |w[9:0] | (|(w[19:10] & thermo)), thermo[t] = (j >= t+1)\n  wire [9:0]  th   = {jj >= 4'd10, jj >= 4'd9, jj >= 4'd8, jj >= 4'd7, jj >= 4'd6,\n                      jj >= 4'd5,  jj >= 4'd4, jj >= 4'd3, jj >= 4'd2, jj >= 4'd1};\n  wire        sts  = (|w[9:0]) | (|(w[19:10] & th)) | tail;\n  wire        rs   = gs & (sts | t10[0]);\n  wire [10:0] fres = {1'b0, t10} + {10'd0, rs};\n  wire        cs   = fres[10];\n\n  // ---- pack ----\n  wire is_nan  = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf  = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (~sub & ovf & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_zero = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | (sub & ~cs & (fres[9:0] == 10'd0)));\n  wire is_sub  = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & ~is_zero & sub;\n\n  assign y = is_nan   ? 16'h7E00 :\n             is_inf   ? {sy, 5'd31, 10'd0} :\n             is_zero  ? {sy, 5'd0,  10'd0} :\n             is_sub   ? {sy, 4'd0, cs, fres[9:0]} :\n                        {sy, expfs[4:0], frac_n};\nendmodule\n", filename='v2.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
v2 passes (151148) — area improved to 96.1 but delay rose to 1573; the critical path now runs through the multiplier with a ~470ps tail of chained flags + output mux. Key insight for v3: the "subnormal value" **already equals signed zero** when `fres==0`, so I can merge the zero/subnormal cases into one select, delete the zero-detect and chained flags, and flatten the pack:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- binary16 multiplier, pre-normalising architecture, v3.\n// v3 vs v2: flattened pack.  The subnormal result value {sign,4'b0,cs,fres}\n// already equals signed zero when fres==0, so zero/subnormal share one select;\n// no separate zero-detect, no chained flags: 3 selects + one 4:1 mux.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // ---- classify ----\n  wire        sa = a[15],    sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10], eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0],   fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0),  eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eap = (ea == 5'd31), ebp = (eb == 5'd31);\n\n  wire zero_a = ea0 & (fa == 10'd0), zero_b = eb0 & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = eap & (fa == 10'd0), inf_b  = ebp & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_in = (eap & (fa != 10'd0)) | (ebp & (fb != 10'd0))\n              | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire sy      = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- significands ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  // ---- 3-level tree leading-zero count (binary, 0..11) ----\n  wire        g0nz = ma[10] | ma[9],  g1nz = ma[8] | ma[7];\n  wire        g2nz = ma[6] | ma[5],   g3nz = ma[4] | ma[3];\n  wire        g4nz = ma[2] | ma[1];\n  wire        g0z = ~ma[10], g1z = ~ma[8], g2z = ~ma[6], g3z = ~ma[4], g4z = ~ma[2];\n  wire        h0nz = g0nz | g1nz;  wire [1:0] h0z = g0nz ? {1'b0, g0z} : {1'b1, g1z};\n  wire        h1nz = g2nz | g3nz;  wire [1:0] h1z = g2nz ? {1'b0, g2z} : {1'b1, g3z};\n  wire        h2nz = g4nz;         wire [1:0] h2z = g4nz ? {1'b0, g4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        f0nz = h0nz | h1nz;  wire [2:0] f0z = h0nz ? {1'b0, h0z} : {1'b1, h1z};\n  wire [3:0]  za = f0nz ? {1'b0, f0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, h2z});\n\n  wire        k0nz = mb[10] | mb[9],  k1nz = mb[8] | mb[7];\n  wire        k2nz = mb[6] | mb[5],   k3nz = mb[4] | mb[3];\n  wire        k4nz = mb[2] | mb[1];\n  wire        k0z = ~mb[10], k1z = ~mb[8], k2z = ~mb[6], k3z = ~mb[4], k4z = ~mb[2];\n  wire        l0nz = k0nz | k1nz;  wire [1:0] l0z = k0nz ? {1'b0, k0z} : {1'b1, k1z};\n  wire        l1nz = k2nz | k3nz;  wire [1:0] l1z = k2nz ? {1'b0, k2z} : {1'b1, k3z};\n  wire        l2nz = k4nz;         wire [1:0] l2z = k4nz ? {1'b0, k4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        m0nz = l0nz | l1nz;  wire [2:0] m0z = l0nz ? {1'b0, l0z} : {1'b1, l1z};\n  wire [3:0]  zb = m0nz ? {1'b0, m0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, l2z});\n\n  // ---- 4-stage binary left shifters (11 bits, drop overflow) ----\n  wire [10:0] sa1 = za[0] ? {ma[9:0],  1'b0} : ma;\n  wire [10:0] sa2 = za[1] ? {sa1[8:0], 2'b0} : sa1;\n  wire [10:0] sa4 = za[2] ? {sa2[6:0], 4'b0} : sa2;\n  wire [10:0] man = za[3] ? {sa4[2:0], 8'b0} : sa4;\n\n  wire [10:0] sb1 = zb[0] ? {mb[9:0],  1'b0} : mb;\n  wire [10:0] sb2 = zb[1] ? {sb1[8:0], 2'b0} : sb1;\n  wire [10:0] sb4 = zb[2] ? {sb2[6:0], 4'b0} : sb2;\n  wire [10:0] mbn = zb[3] ? {sb4[2:0], 8'b0} : sb4;\n\n  // ---- exponent base: jv = -E = 15 - E0 + ZS - s ----\n  wire [5:0]  E0  = {1'b0, (ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea)} + {1'b0, (eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb)};\n  wire [4:0]  ZS  = {1'b0, za} + {1'b0, zb};\n  wire [6:0]  jbase = 7'd15 - {1'b0, E0} + {2'd0, ZS};\n\n  // ---- product, 1-bit normalisation ----\n  wire [21:0] prod = man * mbn;\n  wire        s    = prod[21];\n  wire [20:0] w    = s ? prod[21:1] : prod[20:0];\n  wire        tail = s & prod[0];\n\n  wire [6:0]  jv   = jbase - {6'd0, s};   // jv = -E ; >=0 -> subnormal result\n\n  // ---- normal path: round w at 11-bit precision ----\n  wire [10:0] rpre   = w[20:10];\n  wire        gn     = w[9];\n  wire        stn    = (|w[8:0]) | tail;\n  wire        rup    = gn & (stn | rpre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] rres   = {1'b0, rpre} + {11'd0, rup};\n  wire        cr     = rres[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n = rres[9:0];\n\n  // ---- shared exponent / selection from jv ----\n  wire signed [6:0] expfs = $signed({6'b0, cr}) - $signed(jv);  // E + cr\n  wire sub  = ~jv[6];                       // E <= 0\n  wire ovf  = (expfs >= 7'sd31);            // E + cr >= 31 (only reachable when normal)\n  wire juse = (jv <= 7'd10);                // 0 <= j <= 10\n  wire [3:0] jj = jv[3:0];\n\n  // ---- subnormal path: frac = round(w / 2^(11+j)) ----\n  wire [31:0] wx = {11'd0, w};\n  reg  [11:0] win;\n  always @* begin\n    win = 12'd0;\n    if (juse)\n      case (jj)\n        4'd0:  win = wx[10 +: 12];\n        4'd1:  win = wx[11 +: 12];\n        4'd2:  win = wx[12 +: 12];\n        4'd3:  win = wx[13 +: 12];\n        4'd4:  win = wx[14 +: 12];\n        4'd5:  win = wx[15 +: 12];\n        4'd6:  win = wx[16 +: 12];\n        4'd7:  win = wx[17 +: 12];\n        4'd8:  win = wx[18 +: 12];\n        4'd9:  win = wx[19 +: 12];\n        4'd10: win = wx[20 +: 12];\n        default: win = 12'd0;\n      endcase\n  end\n  wire        gs   = win[0];\n  wire [9:0]  t10  = win[11:1];\n  // sticky = |w[9+j:0] = |w[9:0] | (|(w[19:10] & thermo)), thermo[t] = (j >= t+1)\n  wire [9:0]  th   = {jj >= 4'd10, jj >= 4'd9, jj >= 4'd8, jj >= 4'd7, jj >= 4'd6,\n                      jj >= 4'd5,  jj >= 4'd4, jj >= 4'd3, jj >= 4'd2, jj >= 4'd1};\n  wire        sts  = (|w[9:0]) | (|(w[19:10] & th)) | tail;\n  wire        rs   = gs & (sts | t10[0]);\n  wire [10:0] fres = {1'b0, t10} + {10'd0, rs};\n  wire        cs   = fres[10];\n\n  // ---- pack: priority N > F > (sub|zero) > normal ----\n  wire        v_nan  = nan_in;\n  wire        v_inf  = inf_in | ovf;\n  wire        selA   = ~(v_nan | v_inf);       // not nan, not inf\n  wire        zs     = sub | zero_in;          // subnormal/zero result path\n  wire        selZS  = selA & zs;\n  wire        selNRM = selA & ~zs;\n\n  assign y = v_nan  ? 16'h7E00 :\n             v_inf  ? {sy, 5'd31, 10'd0} :\n             selZS  ? {sy, 4'd0, cs, fres[9:0]} :\n                      {sy, expfs[4:0], frac_n};\nendmodule\n", filename='v3.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
**ADP 135176 — beats the baseline (145429) by 7%.** Now let's push further. The tail still contains ripple incrementers (`t10 + rs`, `rpre + rup`) whose carry chains can be deep. v4: replace both with explicit log-depth (Kogge-Stone style) prefix incrementers:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- binary16 multiplier, pre-normalising architecture, v4.\n// v4 vs v3: log-depth (prefix-AND) incrementers for both rounding adds.\n\nmodule fp_round_inc #(parameter W = 11) (\n  input  [W-1:0] x,\n  input          cin,\n  output [W-1:0] sum,\n  output         cout\n);\n  localparam LOG = 4;  // 2^LOG >= W-1 for W <= 17\n  wire [W-1:0] pre [0:LOG];\n  assign pre[0] = x;\n  genvar k, i;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < LOG; k = k + 1) begin : lvl\n      for (i = 0; i < W; i = i + 1) begin : b\n        if (i >= (1 << k))\n          assign pre[k+1][i] = pre[k][i] & pre[k][i-(1<<k)];\n        else\n          assign pre[k+1][i] = pre[k][i];\n      end\n    end\n    assign sum[0] = x[0] ^ cin;\n    for (i = 1; i < W; i = i + 1) begin : s\n      assign sum[i] = x[i] ^ (cin & pre[LOG][i-1]);\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  assign cout = cin & pre[LOG][W-1];\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // ---- classify ----\n  wire        sa = a[15],    sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10], eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0],   fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0),  eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eap = (ea == 5'd31), ebp = (eb == 5'd31);\n\n  wire zero_a = ea0 & (fa == 10'd0), zero_b = eb0 & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = eap & (fa == 10'd0), inf_b  = ebp & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_in = (eap & (fa != 10'd0)) | (ebp & (fb != 10'd0))\n              | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire sy      = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- significands ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  // ---- 3-level tree leading-zero count (binary, 0..11) ----\n  wire        g0nz = ma[10] | ma[9],  g1nz = ma[8] | ma[7];\n  wire        g2nz = ma[6] | ma[5],   g3nz = ma[4] | ma[3];\n  wire        g4nz = ma[2] | ma[1];\n  wire        g0z = ~ma[10], g1z = ~ma[8], g2z = ~ma[6], g3z = ~ma[4], g4z = ~ma[2];\n  wire        h0nz = g0nz | g1nz;  wire [1:0] h0z = g0nz ? {1'b0, g0z} : {1'b1, g1z};\n  wire        h1nz = g2nz | g3nz;  wire [1:0] h1z = g2nz ? {1'b0, g2z} : {1'b1, g3z};\n  wire        h2nz = g4nz;         wire [1:0] h2z = g4nz ? {1'b0, g4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        f0nz = h0nz | h1nz;  wire [2:0] f0z = h0nz ? {1'b0, h0z} : {1'b1, h1z};\n  wire [3:0]  za = f0nz ? {1'b0, f0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, h2z});\n\n  wire        k0nz = mb[10] | mb[9],  k1nz = mb[8] | mb[7];\n  wire        k2nz = mb[6] | mb[5],   k3nz = mb[4] | mb[3];\n  wire        k4nz = mb[2] | mb[1];\n  wire        k0z = ~mb[10], k1z = ~mb[8], k2z = ~mb[6], k3z = ~mb[4], k4z = ~mb[2];\n  wire        l0nz = k0nz | k1nz;  wire [1:0] l0z = k0nz ? {1'b0, k0z} : {1'b1, k1z};\n  wire        l1nz = k2nz | k3nz;  wire [1:0] l1z = k2nz ? {1'b0, k2z} : {1'b1, k3z};\n  wire        l2nz = k4nz;         wire [1:0] l2z = k4nz ? {1'b0, k4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        m0nz = l0nz | l1nz;  wire [2:0] m0z = l0nz ? {1'b0, l0z} : {1'b1, l1z};\n  wire [3:0]  zb = m0nz ? {1'b0, m0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, l2z});\n\n  // ---- 4-stage binary left shifters (11 bits, drop overflow) ----\n  wire [10:0] sa1 = za[0] ? {ma[9:0],  1'b0} : ma;\n  wire [10:0] sa2 = za[1] ? {sa1[8:0], 2'b0} : sa1;\n  wire [10:0] sa4 = za[2] ? {sa2[6:0], 4'b0} : sa2;\n  wire [10:0] man = za[3] ? {sa4[2:0], 8'b0} : sa4;\n\n  wire [10:0] sb1 = zb[0] ? {mb[9:0],  1'b0} : mb;\n  wire [10:0] sb2 = zb[1] ? {sb1[8:0], 2'b0} : sb1;\n  wire [10:0] sb4 = zb[2] ? {sb2[6:0], 4'b0} : sb2;\n  wire [10:0] mbn = zb[3] ? {sb4[2:0], 8'b0} : sb4;\n\n  // ---- exponent base: jv = -E = 15 - E0 + ZS - s ----\n  wire [5:0]  E0  = {1'b0, (ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea)} + {1'b0, (eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb)};\n  wire [4:0]  ZS  = {1'b0, za} + {1'b0, zb};\n  wire [6:0]  jbase = 7'd15 - {1'b0, E0} + {2'd0, ZS};\n\n  // ---- product, 1-bit normalisation ----\n  wire [21:0] prod = man * mbn;\n  wire        s    = prod[21];\n  wire [20:0] w    = s ? prod[21:1] : prod[20:0];\n  wire        tail = s & prod[0];\n\n  wire [6:0]  jv   = jbase - {6'd0, s};   // jv = -E ; >=0 -> subnormal result\n\n  // ---- normal path: round w at 11-bit precision ----\n  wire [10:0] rpre   = w[20:10];\n  wire        gn     = w[9];\n  wire        stn    = (|w[8:0]) | tail;\n  wire        rup    = gn & (stn | rpre[0]);\n  wire [10:0] rsum;\n  wire        cr;\n  fp_round_inc #(11) u_rnd (.x(rpre), .cin(rup), .sum(rsum), .cout(cr));\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n = rsum[9:0];\n\n  // ---- shared exponent / selection from jv ----\n  wire signed [6:0] expfs = $signed({6'b0, cr}) - $signed(jv);  // E + cr\n  wire sub  = ~jv[6];                       // E <= 0\n  wire ovf  = (expfs >= 7'sd31);            // E + cr >= 31 (only reachable when normal)\n  wire juse = (jv <= 7'd10);                // 0 <= j <= 10\n  wire [3:0] jj = jv[3:0];\n\n  // ---- subnormal path: frac = round(w / 2^(11+j)) ----\n  wire [31:0] wx = {11'd0, w};\n  reg  [11:0] win;\n  always @* begin\n    win = 12'd0;\n    if (juse)\n      case (jj)\n        4'd0:  win = wx[10 +: 12];\n        4'd1:  win = wx[11 +: 12];\n        4'd2:  win = wx[12 +: 12];\n        4'd3:  win = wx[13 +: 12];\n        4'd4:  win = wx[14 +: 12];\n        4'd5:  win = wx[15 +: 12];\n        4'd6:  win = wx[16 +: 12];\n        4'd7:  win = wx[17 +: 12];\n        4'd8:  win = wx[18 +: 12];\n        4'd9:  win = wx[19 +: 12];\n        4'd10: win = wx[20 +: 12];\n        default: win = 12'd0;\n      endcase\n  end\n  wire        gs   = win[0];\n  wire [9:0]  t10  = win[11:1];\n  // sticky = |w[9+j:0] = |w[9:0] | (|(w[19:10] & thermo)), thermo[t] = (j >= t+1)\n  wire [9:0]  th   = {jj >= 4'd10, jj >= 4'd9, jj >= 4'd8, jj >= 4'd7, jj >= 4'd6,\n                      jj >= 4'd5,  jj >= 4'd4, jj >= 4'd3, jj >= 4'd2, jj >= 4'd1};\n  wire        sts  = (|w[9:0]) | (|(w[19:10] & th)) | tail;\n  wire        rs   = gs & (sts | t10[0]);\n  wire [9:0]  fsum;\n  wire        cs;\n  fp_round_inc #(10) u_sub (.x(t10), .cin(rs), .sum(fsum), .cout(cs));\n\n  // ---- pack: priority N > F > (sub|zero) > normal ----\n  wire        v_nan  = nan_in;\n  wire        v_inf  = inf_in | ovf;\n  wire        selA   = ~(v_nan | v_inf);       // not nan, not inf\n  wire        zs     = sub | zero_in;          // subnormal/zero result path\n  wire        selZS  = selA & zs;\n  wire        selNRM = selA & ~zs;\n\n  assign y = v_nan  ? 16'h7E00 :\n             v_inf  ? {sy, 5'd31, 10'd0} :\n             selZS  ? {sy, 4'd0, cs, fsum} :\n                      {sy, expfs[4:0], frac_n};\nendmodule\n", filename='v4.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
v4 slightly worse (136605) — abc's native incrementer was fine; v3 stays best. New idea for v5: **kill the front-end pre-shifters entirely** — multiply raw operands (front = 1 gate), compute LZC off the critical path in parallel with the multiplier, and absorb normalization into a single 22-bit left shifter on the product (`u = prod << ZS`, provably lossless):
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- binary16 multiplier, v5.\n// v5 vs v3: raw multiply (no operand pre-shifters; front-end is 1 gate),\n// normalization moved to a single lossless 22-bit left shifter after the\n// multiplier: u = prod << ZS  where ZS = lz(ma)+lz(mb) (LZC computed in\n// parallel with the multiplier, off the critical path).\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // ---- classify ----\n  wire        sa = a[15],    sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10], eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0],   fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0),  eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eap = (ea == 5'd31), ebp = (eb == 5'd31);\n\n  wire zero_a = ea0 & (fa == 10'd0), zero_b = eb0 & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = eap & (fa == 10'd0), inf_b  = ebp & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_in = (eap & (fa != 10'd0)) | (ebp & (fb != 10'd0))\n              | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire sy      = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- raw significands ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  // ---- tree leading-zero counts (parallel with multiply) ----\n  wire        g0nz = ma[10] | ma[9],  g1nz = ma[8] | ma[7];\n  wire        g2nz = ma[6] | ma[5],   g3nz = ma[4] | ma[3];\n  wire        g4nz = ma[2] | ma[1];\n  wire        g0z = ~ma[10], g1z = ~ma[8], g2z = ~ma[6], g3z = ~ma[4], g4z = ~ma[2];\n  wire        h0nz = g0nz | g1nz;  wire [1:0] h0z = g0nz ? {1'b0, g0z} : {1'b1, g1z};\n  wire        h1nz = g2nz | g3nz;  wire [1:0] h1z = g2nz ? {1'b0, g2z} : {1'b1, g3z};\n  wire        h2nz = g4nz;         wire [1:0] h2z = g4nz ? {1'b0, g4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        f0nz = h0nz | h1nz;  wire [2:0] f0z = h0nz ? {1'b0, h0z} : {1'b1, h1z};\n  wire [3:0]  za = f0nz ? {1'b0, f0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, h2z});\n\n  wire        k0nz = mb[10] | mb[9],  k1nz = mb[8] | mb[7];\n  wire        k2nz = mb[6] | mb[5],   k3nz = mb[4] | mb[3];\n  wire        k4nz = mb[2] | mb[1];\n  wire        k0z = ~mb[10], k1z = ~mb[8], k2z = ~mb[6], k3z = ~mb[4], k4z = ~mb[2];\n  wire        l0nz = k0nz | k1nz;  wire [1:0] l0z = k0nz ? {1'b0, k0z} : {1'b1, k1z};\n  wire        l1nz = k2nz | k3nz;  wire [1:0] l1z = k2nz ? {1'b0, k2z} : {1'b1, k3z};\n  wire        l2nz = k4nz;         wire [1:0] l2z = k4nz ? {1'b0, k4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        m0nz = l0nz | l1nz;  wire [2:0] m0z = l0nz ? {1'b0, l0z} : {1'b1, l1z};\n  wire [3:0]  zb = m0nz ? {1'b0, m0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, l2z});\n\n  wire [4:0]  ZS  = {1'b0, za} + {1'b0, zb};      // 0..22\n\n  // ---- exponent base: jv = -E = 15 - E0 + ZS - s ----\n  wire [5:0]  E0  = {1'b0, (ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea)} + {1'b0, (eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb)};\n  wire [6:0]  jbase = 7'd15 - {1'b0, E0} + {2'd0, ZS};\n\n  // ---- raw product ----\n  wire [21:0] prod = ma * mb;\n\n  // ---- normalise: u = prod << ZS (lossless: prod < 2^(22-ZS)) ----\n  wire [21:0] u1 = ZS[0] ? {prod[20:0], 1'b0} : prod;\n  wire [21:0] u2 = ZS[1] ? {u1[19:0], 2'b0}   : u1;\n  wire [21:0] u4 = ZS[2] ? {u2[17:0], 4'b0}   : u2;\n  wire [21:0] u8 = ZS[3] ? {u4[13:0], 8'b0}   : u4;\n  wire [21:0] u  = ZS[4] ? {u8[5:0], 16'b0}   : u8;\n\n  wire        s    = u[21];\n  wire [20:0] w    = s ? u[21:1] : u[20:0];\n  wire        tail = s & u[0];\n\n  wire [6:0]  jv   = jbase - {6'd0, s};   // jv = -E ; >=0 -> subnormal result\n\n  // ---- normal path: round w at 11-bit precision ----\n  wire [10:0] rpre   = w[20:10];\n  wire        gn     = w[9];\n  wire        stn    = (|w[8:0]) | tail;\n  wire        rup    = gn & (stn | rpre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] rres   = {1'b0, rpre} + {11'd0, rup};\n  wire        cr     = rres[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n = rres[9:0];\n\n  // ---- shared exponent / selection from jv ----\n  wire signed [6:0] expfs = $signed({6'b0, cr}) - $signed(jv);  // E + cr\n  wire sub  = ~jv[6];                       // E <= 0\n  wire ovf  = (expfs >= 7'sd31);            // E + cr >= 31 (only reachable when normal)\n  wire juse = (jv <= 7'd10);                // 0 <= j <= 10\n  wire [3:0] jj = jv[3:0];\n\n  // ---- subnormal path: frac = round(w / 2^(11+j)) ----\n  wire [31:0] wx = {11'd0, w};\n  reg  [11:0] win;\n  always @* begin\n    win = 12'd0;\n    if (juse)\n      case (jj)\n        4'd0:  win = wx[10 +: 12];\n        4'd1:  win = wx[11 +: 12];\n        4'd2:  win = wx[12 +: 12];\n        4'd3:  win = wx[13 +: 12];\n        4'd4:  win = wx[14 +: 12];\n        4'd5:  win = wx[15 +: 12];\n        4'd6:  win = wx[16 +: 12];\n        4'd7:  win = wx[17 +: 12];\n        4'd8:  win = wx[18 +: 12];\n        4'd9:  win = wx[19 +: 12];\n        4'd10: win = wx[20 +: 12];\n        default: win = 12'd0;\n      endcase\n  end\n  wire        gs   = win[0];\n  wire [9:0]  t10  = win[11:1];\n  // sticky = |w[9+j:0] = |w[9:0] | (|(w[19:10] & thermo)), thermo[t] = (j >= t+1)\n  wire [9:0]  th   = {jj >= 4'd10, jj >= 4'd9, jj >= 4'd8, jj >= 4'd7, jj >= 4'd6,\n                      jj >= 4'd5,  jj >= 4'd4, jj >= 4'd3, jj >= 4'd2, jj >= 4'd1};\n  wire        sts  = (|w[9:0]) | (|(w[19:10] & th)) | tail;\n  wire        rs   = gs & (sts | t10[0]);\n  wire [10:0] fres = {1'b0, t10} + {10'd0, rs};\n  wire        cs   = fres[10];\n\n  // ---- pack: priority N > F > (sub|zero) > normal ----\n  wire        v_nan  = nan_in;\n  wire        v_inf  = inf_in | ovf;\n  wire        selA   = ~(v_nan | v_inf);       // not nan, not inf\n  wire        zs     = sub | zero_in;          // subnormal/zero result path\n  wire        selZS  = selA & zs;\n  wire        selNRM = selA & ~zs;\n\n  assign y = v_nan  ? 16'h7E00 :\n             v_inf  ? {sy, 5'd31, 10'd0} :\n             selZS  ? {sy, 4'd0, cs, fres[9:0]} :\n                      {sy, expfs[4:0], frac_n};\nendmodule\n", filename='v5.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
**ADP 125435 — 13.7% better than baseline.** Now the subnormal path stalls waiting for `s = u[21]` (only available after the product shifter). But `s = u[21] = prod[21-ZS]` can be extracted right after the multiplier with a small 22:1 mux, freeing `jv/jj/th` ~150ps earlier:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- binary16 multiplier, v6.\n// v6 vs v5: s extracted early as prod[21-ZS] (small 22:1 mux right after the\n// multiply) instead of u[21] after the normalisation shifter, so jv/jj/thermo\n// are ready before the shifter output w arrives.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // ---- classify ----\n  wire        sa = a[15],    sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10], eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0],   fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0),  eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eap = (ea == 5'd31), ebp = (eb == 5'd31);\n\n  wire zero_a = ea0 & (fa == 10'd0), zero_b = eb0 & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = eap & (fa == 10'd0), inf_b  = ebp & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_in = (eap & (fa != 10'd0)) | (ebp & (fb != 10'd0))\n              | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire sy      = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- raw significands ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  // ---- tree leading-zero counts (parallel with multiply) ----\n  wire        g0nz = ma[10] | ma[9],  g1nz = ma[8] | ma[7];\n  wire        g2nz = ma[6] | ma[5],   g3nz = ma[4] | ma[3];\n  wire        g4nz = ma[2] | ma[1];\n  wire        g0z = ~ma[10], g1z = ~ma[8], g2z = ~ma[6], g3z = ~ma[4], g4z = ~ma[2];\n  wire        h0nz = g0nz | g1nz;  wire [1:0] h0z = g0nz ? {1'b0, g0z} : {1'b1, g1z};\n  wire        h1nz = g2nz | g3nz;  wire [1:0] h1z = g2nz ? {1'b0, g2z} : {1'b1, g3z};\n  wire        h2nz = g4nz;         wire [1:0] h2z = g4nz ? {1'b0, g4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        f0nz = h0nz | h1nz;  wire [2:0] f0z = h0nz ? {1'b0, h0z} : {1'b1, h1z};\n  wire [3:0]  za = f0nz ? {1'b0, f0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, h2z});\n\n  wire        k0nz = mb[10] | mb[9],  k1nz = mb[8] | mb[7];\n  wire        k2nz = mb[6] | mb[5],   k3nz = mb[4] | mb[3];\n  wire        k4nz = mb[2] | mb[1];\n  wire        k0z = ~mb[10], k1z = ~mb[8], k2z = ~mb[6], k3z = ~mb[4], k4z = ~mb[2];\n  wire        l0nz = k0nz | k1nz;  wire [1:0] l0z = k0nz ? {1'b0, k0z} : {1'b1, k1z};\n  wire        l1nz = k2nz | k3nz;  wire [1:0] l1z = k2nz ? {1'b0, k2z} : {1'b1, k3z};\n  wire        l2nz = k4nz;         wire [1:0] l2z = k4nz ? {1'b0, k4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        m0nz = l0nz | l1nz;  wire [2:0] m0z = l0nz ? {1'b0, l0z} : {1'b1, l1z};\n  wire [3:0]  zb = m0nz ? {1'b0, m0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, l2z});\n\n  wire [4:0]  ZS  = {1'b0, za} + {1'b0, zb};      // 0..22\n\n  // ---- exponent base: jv = -E = 15 - E0 + ZS - s ----\n  wire [5:0]  E0  = {1'b0, (ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea)} + {1'b0, (eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb)};\n  wire [6:0]  jbase = 7'd15 - {1'b0, E0} + {2'd0, ZS};\n\n  // ---- raw product ----\n  wire [21:0] prod = ma * mb;\n\n  // ---- early s:  s = u[21] = prod[21-ZS]  (ZS==22 -> prod==0 -> s==0) ----\n  reg s_pre;\n  integer ki;\n  always @* begin\n    s_pre = 1'b0;\n    for (ki = 0; ki <= 21; ki = ki + 1)\n      if (ZS == ki[4:0]) s_pre = prod[ki];\n  end\n  wire s = s_pre;\n\n  // ---- normalise: u = prod << ZS (lossless: prod < 2^(22-ZS)) ----\n  wire [21:0] u1 = ZS[0] ? {prod[20:0], 1'b0} : prod;\n  wire [21:0] u2 = ZS[1] ? {u1[19:0], 2'b0}   : u1;\n  wire [21:0] u4 = ZS[2] ? {u2[17:0], 4'b0}   : u2;\n  wire [21:0] u8 = ZS[3] ? {u4[13:0], 8'b0}   : u4;\n  wire [21:0] u  = ZS[4] ? {u8[5:0], 16'b0}   : u8;\n\n  wire [20:0] w    = s ? u[21:1] : u[20:0];\n  wire        tail = s & u[0];\n\n  wire [6:0]  jv   = jbase - {6'd0, s};   // jv = -E ; >=0 -> subnormal result\n\n  // ---- normal path: round w at 11-bit precision ----\n  wire [10:0] rpre   = w[20:10];\n  wire        gn     = w[9];\n  wire        stn    = (|w[8:0]) | tail;\n  wire        rup    = gn & (stn | rpre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] rres   = {1'b0, rpre} + {11'd0, rup};\n  wire        cr     = rres[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n = rres[9:0];\n\n  // ---- shared exponent / selection from jv ----\n  wire signed [6:0] expfs = $signed({6'b0, cr}) - $signed(jv);       // not nan, not inf\n  wire        zs     = sub | zero_in;          // subnormal/zero result path\n  wire        selZS  = selA & zs;\n  wire        selNRM = selA & ~zs;\n\n  assign y = v_nan  ? 16'h7E00 :\n             v_inf  ? {sy, 5'd31, 10'd0} :\n             selZS  ? {sy, 4'd0, cs, fres[9:0]} :\n                      {sy, expfs[4:0], frac_n};\nendmodule\n", filename='v6.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
v6 worse (132471): the 22:1 mux cost area without real delay gain. v5 (125435) stays best. 

New idea for v7: in v5 the win/sticky select `jj = (jbase - s)` stalls until `s` arrives after the shifter. Instead compute **two parallel branches** (s=0 and s=1) whose selects `jbase`, `jbase-1` are ready at ~230ps, and mux branch results by `s` afterwards:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- binary16 multiplier, v7.\n// v7 vs v5: dual-branch subnormal path.  The window select jj = (jbase-s)\n// previously waited for s (after the normalisation shifter).  Now both\n// branches (s=0: jj=jbase, s=1: jj=jbase-1) are computed in parallel with\n// selects available early; results are muxed by s afterwards.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // ---- classify ----\n  wire        sa = a[15],    sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10], eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0],   fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0),  eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eap = (ea == 5'd31), ebp = (eb == 5'd31);\n\n  wire zero_a = ea0 & (fa == 10'd0), zero_b = eb0 & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = eap & (fa == 10'd0), inf_b  = ebp & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_in = (eap & (fa != 10'd0)) | (ebp & (fb != 10'd0))\n              | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire sy      = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- raw significands ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  // ---- tree leading-zero counts (parallel with multiply) ----\n  wire        g0nz = ma[10] | ma[9],  g1nz = ma[8] | ma[7];\n  wire        g2nz = ma[6] | ma[5],   g3nz = ma[4] | ma[3];\n  wire        g4nz = ma[2] | ma[1];\n  wire        g0z = ~ma[10], g1z = ~ma[8], g2z = ~ma[6], g3z = ~ma[4], g4z = ~ma[2];\n  wire        h0nz = g0nz | g1nz;  wire [1:0] h0z = g0nz ? {1'b0, g0z} : {1'b1, g1z};\n  wire        h1nz = g2nz | g3nz;  wire [1:0] h1z = g2nz ? {1'b0, g2z} : {1'b1, g3z};\n  wire        h2nz = g4nz;         wire [1:0] h2z = g4nz ? {1'b0, g4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        f0nz = h0nz | h1nz;  wire [2:0] f0z = h0nz ? {1'b0, h0z} : {1'b1, h1z};\n  wire [3:0]  za = f0nz ? {1'b0, f0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, h2z});\n\n  wire        k0nz = mb[10] | mb[9],  k1nz = mb[8] | mb[7];\n  wire        k2nz = mb[6] | mb[5],   k3nz = mb[4] | mb[3];\n  wire        k4nz = mb[2] | mb[1];\n  wire        k0z = ~mb[10], k1z = ~mb[8], k2z = ~mb[6], k3z = ~mb[4], k4z = ~mb[2];\n  wire        l0nz = k0nz | k1nz;  wire [1:0] l0z = k0nz ? {1'b0, k0z} : {1'b1, k1z};\n  wire        l1nz = k2nz | k3nz;  wire [1:0] l1z = k2nz ? {1'b0, k2z} : {1'b1, k3z};\n  wire        l2nz = k4nz;         wire [1:0] l2z = k4nz ? {1'b0, k4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        m0nz = l0nz | l1nz;  wire [2:0] m0z = l0nz ? {1'b0, l0z} : {1'b1, l1z};\n  wire [3:0]  zb = m0nz ? {1'b0, m0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, l2z});\n\n  wire [4:0]  ZS  = {1'b0, za} + {1'b0, zb};      // 0..22\n\n  // ---- exponent base: jv = -E = 15 - E0 + ZS - s ----\n  wire [5:0]  E0  = {1'b0, (ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea)} + {1'b0, (eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb)};\n  wire [6:0]  jbase = 7'd15 - {1'b0, E0} + {2'd0, ZS};\n  wire [6:0]  jv1  = jbase - 7'd1;\n\n  // ---- raw product ----\n  wire [21:0] prod = ma * mb;\n\n  // ---- normalise: u = prod << ZS (lossless: prod < 2^(22-ZS)) ----\n  wire [21:0] u1 = ZS[0] ? {prod[20:0], 1'b0} : prod;\n  wire [21:0] u2 = ZS[1] ? {u1[19:0], 2'b0}   : u1;\n  wire [21:0] u4 = ZS[2] ? {u2[17:0], 4'b0}   : u2;\n  wire [21:0] u8 = ZS[3] ? {u4[13:0], 8'b0}   : u4;\n  wire [21:0] u  = ZS[4] ? {u8[5:0], 16'b0}   : u8;\n\n  wire        s    = u[21];\n  wire [20:0] w    = s ? u[21:1] : u[20:0];\n  wire        tail = s & u[0];\n\n  wire [6:0]  jv   = s ? jv1 : jbase;      // jv = -E ; >=0 -> subnormal result\n\n  // ---- normal path: round w at 11-bit precision ----\n  wire [10:0] rpre   = w[20:10];\n  wire        gn     = w[9];\n  wire        stn    = (|w[8:0]) | tail;\n  wire        rup    = gn & (stn | rpre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] rres   = {1'b0, rpre} + {11'd0, rup};\n  wire        cr     = rres[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n = rres[9:0];\n\n  // ---- shared exponent / selection from jv ----\n  wire signed [6:0] expfs = $signed({6'b0, cr}) - $signed(jv);  // E + cr\n  wire sub  = ~jv[6];                       // E <= 0\n  wire ovf  = (expfs >= 7'sd31);            // E + cr >= 31 (only reachable when normal)\n\n  // ---- subnormal path, two branches (s=0 / s=1), selects ready early ----\n  wire [32:0] ux  = {11'd0, u};\n  wire        use0 = (jbase <= 7'd10);\n  wire        use1 = (jv1   <= 7'd10);\n  wire [3:0]  jj0  = jbase[3:0];\n  wire [3:0]  jj1  = jv1[3:0];\n\n  reg [11:0] win0, win1;\n  integer    ki;\n  always @* begin\n    win0 = 12'd0;\n    if (use0)\n      for (ki = 0; ki <= 10; ki = ki + 1)\n        if (jj0 == ki[3:0]) win0 = ux[10+ki +: 12];\n    win1 = 12'd0;\n    if (use1)\n      for (ki = 0; ki <= 10; ki = ki + 1)\n        if (jj1 == ki[3:0]) win1 = ux[11+ki +: 12];\n  end\n\n  // thermo masks: branch0 over u[19:10], branch1 over u[20:10]\n  wire [9:0]  th0 = {jj0 >= 4'd10, jj0 >= 4'd9, jj0 >= 4'd8, jj0 >= 4'd7, jj0 >= 4'd6,\n                     jj0 >= 4'd5,  jj0 >= 4'd4, jj0 >= 4'd3, jj0 >= 4'd2, jj0 >= 4'd1};\n  wire [10:0] th1 = {jj1 >= 4'd10, jj1 >= 4'd9, jj1 >= 4'd8, jj1 >= 4'd7, jj1 >= 4'd6,\n                     jj1 >= 4'd5,  jj1 >= 4'd4, jj1 >= 4'd3, jj1 >= 4'd2, jj1 >= 4'd1,\n                     1'b1};\n  wire sts0 = (|u[9:0])  | (|(u[19:10] & th0));\n  wire sts1 = (|u[9:0])  | (|(u[20:10] & th1));\n\n  // select branch by s\n  wire [11:0] win  = s ? win1 : win0;\n  wire        sts  = s ? sts1 : sts0;\n\n  wire        gs   = win[0];\n  wire [9:0]  t10  = win[11:1];\n  wire        rs   = gs & (sts | t10[0]);\n  wire [10:0] fres = {1'b0, t10} + {10'd0, rs};\n  wire        cs   = fres[10];\n\n  // ---- pack: priority N > F > (sub|zero) > normal ----\n  wire        v_nan  = nan_in;\n  wire        v_inf  = inf_in | ovf;\n  wire        selA   = ~(v_nan | v_inf);       // not nan, not inf\n  wire        zs     = sub | zero_in;          // subnormal/zero result path\n  wire        selZS  = selA & zs;\n  wire        selNRM = selA & ~zs;\n\n  assign y = v_nan  ? 16'h7E00 :\n             v_inf  ? {sy, 5'd31, 10'd0} :\n             selZS  ? {sy, 4'd0, cs, fres[9:0]} :\n                      {sy, expfs[4:0], frac_n};\nendmodule\n", filename='v7.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
**ADP 114570 — 21% under baseline** (explicit carry-outs paid off). Now the tail's sticky OR-tree still waits for the post-shifter `u`. But sticky can be computed in the **product domain**: `sts_k = |prod[(9+k+jj_k-ZS) : 0]` via a thermo mask on threshold `e_k = 9+k+jj_k-ZS`, ready right after the multiply:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- binary16 multiplier, v9.\n// v9 vs v7(+explicit carries): subnormal sticky moved to the product domain:\n//   sts_k = |u[k +: 10+jj_k] = |prod[e_k : 0],  e_k = 9+k+jj_k - ZS\n// so the sticky OR-tree starts right after the multiply instead of waiting\n// for the normalisation shifter output u.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // ---- classify ----\n  wire        sa = a[15],    sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10], eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0],   fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0),  eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eap = (ea == 5'd31), ebp = (eb == 5'd31);\n\n  wire zero_a = ea0 & (fa == 10'd0), zero_b = eb0 & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = eap & (fa == 10'd0), inf_b  = ebp & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_in = (eap & (fa != 10'd0)) | (ebp & (fb != 10'd0))\n              | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire sy      = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- raw significands ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  // ---- tree leading-zero counts (parallel with multiply) ----\n  wire        g0nz = ma[10] | ma[9],  g1nz = ma[8] | ma[7];\n  wire        g2nz = ma[6] | ma[5],   g3nz = ma[4] | ma[3];\n  wire        g4nz = ma[2] | ma[1];\n  wire        g0z = ~ma[10], g1z = ~ma[8], g2z = ~ma[6], g3z = ~ma[4], g4z = ~ma[2];\n  wire        h0nz = g0nz | g1nz;  wire [1:0] h0z = g0nz ? {1'b0, g0z} : {1'b1, g1z};\n  wire        h1nz = g2nz | g3nz;  wire [1:0] h1z = g2nz ? {1'b0, g2z} : {1'b1, g3z};\n  wire        h2nz = g4nz;         wire [1:0] h2z = g4nz ? {1'b0, g4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        f0nz = h0nz | h1nz;                       // E <= 0\n  wire ovf  = (expfs >= 7'sd31);            // E + cr >= 31 (only reachable when normal)\n\n  // ---- subnormal path, two branches (s=0 / s=1), selects ready early ----\n  wire [32:0] ux  = {11'd0, u};\n  wire        use0 = (jbase <= 7'd10);\n  wire        use1 = (jv1   <= 7'd10);\n  wire [3:0]  jj0  = jbase[3:0];\n  wire [3:0]  jj1  = jv1[3:0];\n\n  reg [11:0] win0, win1;\n  integer    ki;\n  always @* begin\n    win0 = 12'd0;\n    if (use0)\n      for (ki = 0; ki <= 10; ki = ki + 1)\n        if (jj0 == ki[3:0]) win0 = ux[10+ki +: 12];\n    win1 = 12'd0;\n    if (use1)\n      for (ki = 0; ki <= 10; ki = ki + 1)\n        if (jj1 == ki[3:0]) win1 = ux[11+ki +: 12];\n  end\n\n  // ---- sticky in product domain: sts_k = |prod[e_k : 0], e_k = 9+k+jj_k-ZS ----\n  wire signed [5:0] e0 = $signed({2'b00, jj0}) + 6'sd9  - $signed({1'b0, ZS});\n  wire signed [5:0] e1 = $signed({2'b00, jj1}) + 6'sd10 - $signed({1'b0, ZS});\n  wire [21:0] mk0 = {{22{e0 >= 6'sd21}}, {22{e0 >= 6'sd20}}, 20'd0} ; // placeholder\n  // build masks bit by bit\n  wire [21:0] msk0, msk1;\n  genvar gt;\n  generate\n    for (gt = 0; gt < 22; gt = gt + 1) begin : g_msk\n      assign msk0[gt] = (e0 >= $signed({1'b0, gt[4:0]}));\n      assign msk1[gt] = (e1 >= $signed({1'b0, gt[4:0]}));\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  wire sts0 = |(prod & msk0);\n  wire sts1 = |(prod & msk1);\n\n  // select branch by s\n  wire [11:0] win  = s ? win1 : win0;\n  wire        sts  = s ? sts1 : sts0;\n\n  wire        gs   = win[0];\n  wire [9:0]  t10  = win[11:1];\n  wire        rs   = gs & (sts | t10[0]);\n  wire [10:0] fres = {1'b0, t10} + {10'd0, rs};\n  wire        cs   = rs & (&t10); [ASSISTANT]
v9 (prod-domain sticky) is worse — masks too costly; v7.sv (114570) remains best. Path analysis shows the critical path is **front (LZC+ZS-add, 400ps) → ZS shifter (250ps) → win**, bypassing the multiplier. v10: move normalization back to the operands (v3-style pre-shift), which **eliminates the ZS shifter entirely** — windows come straight from `prod`:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- binary16 multiplier, v10.\n// v10: pre-normalising operands (4-stage shifters before the multiply) so the\n// product is already normalised -- the ZS normalisation shifter after the\n// multiply disappears; win/sticky extract straight from prod. {1'b0, g0z} : {1'b1, g1z};\n  wire        h1nz = g2nz | g3nz;  wire [1:0] h1z = g2nz ? {1'b0, g2z} : {1'b1, g3z};\n  wire        h2nz = g4nz;         wire [1:0] h2z = g4nz ? {1'b0, g4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        f0nz = h0nz | h1nz;  wire [2:0] f0z = h0nz ? {1'b0, k0z} : {1'b1, k1z};\n  wire        l1nz = k2nz | k3nz;  wire [1:0] l1z = k2nz ? {1'b0, k2z} : {1'b1, k3z};\n  wire        l2nz = k4nz;         wire [1:0] l2z = k4nz ? {1'b0, k4z} : 2'd2;\n  wire        m0nz = l0nz | l1nz;  wire [2:0] m0z = l0nz ? {1'b0, l0z} : {1'b1, l1z};\n  wire [3:0]  zb = m0nz ? {1'b0, m0z} : (4'd8 + {1'b0, l2z});\n\n  // ---- 4-stage binary left shifters (11 bits, drop overflow) ----\n  wire [10:0] sa1 = za[0] ? {ma[9:0],  1'b0} : ma;\n  wire [10:0] sa2 = za[1] ? win1 : win0;\n  wire        sts  = s ? sts1 : sts0;\n\n  wire        gs   = win[0];\n  wire [9:0]  t10  = win[11:1];\n  wire        rs   = gs & (sts | t10[0]);\n  wire [10:0] fres = {1'b0, t10} + {10'd0, rs};\n  wire        cs   = rs & (&t10);   // explicit carry-out (AND tree)\n\n  // ---- pack: priority N > F > (sub|zero) > normal ----\n  wire        v_nan  = nan_in;\n  wire        v_inf  = inf_in | ovf;\n  wire        selA   = ~(v_nan | v_inf);       // not nan, not inf\n  wire        zs     = sub | zero_in;          // subnormal/zero result path\n  wire        selZS  = selA & zs;\n  wire        selNRM = selA & ~zs;\n\n  assign y = v_nan  ? **ADP 102122** (area 95.0, delay 1075) — 30% under baseline. The tail now looks like the *normal* rounding path: `w → stn/rup → ripple carry to bit 8 → mux`. v13: replace both native `+` adds with the log-depth prefix incrementers (now that carries are actually critical):
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- binary16 multiplier, v13.\n// v13 vs v12: log-depth (prefix-AND) incrementers for both rounding adds;\n// carry-outs taken from the module (== rup&(&rpre), rs&(&t10)).\n\nmodule fp_round_inc #(parameter W = 11) (\n  input  [W-1:0] x,\n  input          cin,\n  output [W-1:0] sum,\n  output         cout\n);\n  localparam LOG = 4;  // 2^LOG >= W-1 for W <= 17\n  wire [W-1:0] pre [0:LOG];\n  assign pre[0] = x;\n  genvar k, i;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < LOG; k = k + 1) begin : lvl\n      for (i = 0; i < W; i = i + 1) begin : b\n        if (i >= (1 << k))\n          assign pre[k+1][i] = pre[k][i] & pre[k][i-(1<<k)];\n        else\n          assign pre[k+1][i] = pre[k][i];\n      end\n    end\n    assign sum[0] = x[0] ^ cin;\n    for (i = 1; i < W; i = i + 1) begin : s\n      assign sum[i] = x[i] ^ (cin & pre[LOG][i-1]);\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  assign cout = cin & pre[LOG][W-1];\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  // ---- classify ----\n  wire        sa = a[15],    sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10], eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0],   fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0),  eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eap = (ea == 5'd31), ebp = (eb == 5'd31);\n\n  wire zero_a = ea0 & (fa == 10'd0), zero_b = eb0 & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = eap & (fa == 10'd0), inf_b  = ebp & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_in = (eap & (fa != 10'd0)) | (ebp & (fb != 10'd0))\n              | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire sy      = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- raw significands ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  // ---- tree leading-zero counts (parallel with multiply) ----\n  wire        g0nz = ma[10] | ma[9],  g1nz = ma[8] | ma[7];\n  wire        g2nz = ma[6] | ma[5],   g3nz = ma[4] | ma[3];\n  wire        g4nz = ma[2] | ma[1];\n  wire        g0z = ~ma[10], g1z = ~ma[8], g2z = ~ma[6], g3z = ~ma[4], g4z = ~ma[2];\n  wire        h0nz = g0nz | g1nz; The run is complete. Final design: **`v14.sv`** — 100% correct on all 18,874,371 testbench checks, with **area_delay_product = 99,513** vs. the starting point's 145,429 — a **31.6% improvement** (area 97.28, delay 1023 ps).

## Summary of the optimization journey

| Version | Key change | ADP |
|---|---|---|
| starting_point | unpack→mult→LZC(22)→barrel shift→round | 145,429 |
| v1 | operand pre-normalization, tree LZC, subnormal path | 152,320 |
| v2 | tree LZC + binary shifters, `jv`-centric exponent math | 151,148 |
| v3 | flattened pack (subnormal value ≡ signed-zero when `fres==0`) | **135,177** |
| v5 | **raw multiply** + lossless `u = prod << ZS` post-shifter | **125,435** |
| v7 | dual-branch (s=0/s=1) subnormal window, selects ready early | **121,129** |
| v7+ | explicit `cr/cs` carry-out AND-trees | **114,570** |
| v11 | overflow derived directly from `(jv, cr)` (no `expfs` compare) | **109,596** |
| v12 | single window/sticky via `jj0 = jj1+1` identity (halves shifter) | **102,122** |
| v14 | keep-duplicated `ea0/eb0` classify drivers (fanout split) | 101,859 |
| v15 | keep-duplicated `rup/rs` carry-out drivers | 101,509 |
| **v16 (=v14.sv final)** | **keep-duplicated `ZS` shifter-control adder** | **99,513** |

Key structural insights that mattered: moving normalization from a post-multiply 22-bit barrel shifter to a single lossless left-shift; deriving every rounding/selection quantity from one `jv = -E`; proving the two subnormal shift branches collapse to one extraction; computing carry-outs and overflow as cheap direct expressions; and using `(* keep *)` driver duplication to balance fanout on the classify/`ZS`/round nodes. Rejected along the way (reverted after measurement): prod-domain sticky masks, prefix incrementers, pre-normalized operand paths, early-`s` muxes, and `jbase` duplication — each measured before being kept.

## Summary

**Final: PASS, ADP = 99,513** (best file: `v14.sv`, area 97.3, delay 1023 ps) vs. baseline 145,429 — a **31.6% improvement**.

### 1. Approaches tried / what worked
- Started from `starting_point.v` (unpack → 11×11 multiply → 22-bit LZC + bidirectional barrel shifter + dual rounding paths).
- Built a **pre-normalizing architecture** first (v1–v4): LZC + shift operands before the multiply. Correct but slower — the front-end sat in front of the multiplier.
- Pivoted to **raw multiply + one lossless `u = prod << ZS` post-shifter** (v5) — LZC moves off the critical path (parallel with the multiply). This was the single biggest architectural win (135k → 125k).
- Then a series of targeted refinements (v7–v16), each measured: dual-branch subnormal window, direct overflow derivation, single-extraction identity, fanout duplication.

### 2. Most impactful optimizations
1. **Flattened pack** (v3): realized the subnormal result value `{sign,4'b0,cs,fres}` already equals signed zero when `fres==0`, so zero/subnormal share one select — deleted the zero-detect and chained flags (−10k).
2. **Raw multiply + `prod<<ZS` normalizer** (v5) — removed the LZC from the input path (−10k).
3. **Dual-branch window select** (v7): `jj = jbase-s` waited for `s`; computing both `s=0/s=1` branches with early selects (−4k).
4. **Direct `cr/cs` carry-outs** as AND-trees, and **`ovf` from `(jv, cr)`** instead of a full subtract+compare (−5k, −5k).
5. **`jj0 = jj1+1` identity** (v12): both subnormal branches use the *same* window extraction and sticky range — halved the window logic (−7k).
6. **`(* keep *)` driver duplication** of `ea0/eb0`, `rup/rs`, and especially `ZS` (fanout split between shifter control and exponent math) — small, cumulative wins to 99.5k.

### 3. What didn't work (reverted)
- Pre-normalized operands with v7-style tail (v10) — serializes front + multiply.
- Prod-domain sticky with thermo masks (v9) — +6 area, slower.
- Log-depth prefix incrementers (v4, v13) — abc's native `+` maps better; made things worse twice.
- Early-`s` via 22:1 `prod[21-ZS]` mux (v6), `jbase` duplication (v17), `s` self-AND buffer (v18), multiplier operand swap — all regressed or were area-neutral losses.
- `target_delay` had **zero** effect on mapping in this flow (tested 500 vs 1500).

### 4. Lessons learned
- **Measure everything**; synthesis results were often counterintuitive (e.g., explicit fast structures losing to abc's native inference). Each change was evaluated in isolation so reverts were cheap.
- **Path reports drove every decision**: decomposing the critical path segment-by-segment (front / mult / shifter / window / sticky / round / pack) showed where the real time went — my initial analysis (blaming the LZC) was wrong until the path was read carefully.
- Balancing *parallel* branches matters more than making one branch fastest: several "faster" sub-branches (v9 sticky) just moved the bottleneck.
- Fanout is a first-class delay lever at this scale; `keep`-guarded duplicate drivers were the cheapest late wins.
- Next time I'd restructure the pack/overflow logic and fanout splits *early* (they were nearly free wins found late), and spend the budget on the two inherently deep blocks (multiplier CPA, 5-stage shifter) only after the tail is lean.
